{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "91b85531-839e-46a4-be97-c2e961fa4b79",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "4129aada-563b-4840-9ffb-6d88c51600ca",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "bef7b862-83cc-46c5-b466-8b181f8a361c",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex",
    "oldString": "the training regime (SFT vs.\\ RL) qualitatively changes",
    "newString": "the training regime (supervised fine-tuning, SFT, vs.\\ reinforcement learning, RL) qualitatively changes",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\nContamCal operates in two stages: Stage~1 estimates per-item contamination exposure from multiple detection signals via training-free fusion; Stage~2 embeds these scores in a contamination-augmented IRT model to produce calibrated ability estimates with credible intervals.\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nLet $y_{ijk} \\in \\{0,1\\}$ denote the binary response of model~$j$ ($j=1,\\ldots,J$) to item~$i$ ($i=1,\\ldots,I_k$) on benchmark~$k$. Standard IRT assumes $P(y_{ijk}=1) = f(\\theta_j, \\boldsymbol{\\xi}_i)$ where $\\theta_j$ is latent ability and $\\boldsymbol{\\xi}_i$ collects item parameters. When training corpora contain benchmark items, the observed ability $\\hat{\\theta}_j$ confounds genuine capability with memorization.\n\nOur goal is twofold: (1)~estimate $\\theta_j^{\\text{clean}}$, the ability each model would exhibit had it never encountered test items during training, and (2)~diagnose contamination effects at domain granularity within each benchmark. We draw on item compromise IRT \\citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge} and explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}, adapting these frameworks through three structural modifications for LLM evaluation.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~1: Independent examinees $\\to$ shared item pool.} Classical IRT assumes independent test-takers. In LLM evaluation, a fixed item set is shared across all models, and responses within a single model may exhibit residual correlations beyond the scalar~$\\theta_j$ \\citep{sanmartin_2009_irt}. We mitigate this by fitting per-benchmark models.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~2: Binary leakage $\\to$ continuous exposure.} Item compromise IRT uses binary indicators \\citep{sinharay_2017_preknowledge}. LLM contamination is graded: a model may have encountered an item zero, once, or hundreds of times. We replace binary indicators with continuous exposure scores $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$ derived from detection signals, following the explanatory IRT tradition \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}.\n\n\\textit{Adaptation~3: Stable ability $\\to$ regime-conditional ability.} In educational testing, ability is approximately constant. For LLMs, the training regime (SFT vs.\\ RL) qualitatively changes both internal representations and signal reliability. We recover clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting exposure to zero) rather than regime-specific ability parameters.\n\n\\subsection{Stage 1: Multi-Signal Exposure Mapping}\n\\label{sec:stage1}\n\nFor each model~$j$ and item~$i$, we consider four candidate detection signals. \\textbf{S1}~(Embedding similarity): cosine similarity between the item embedding and its nearest neighbor in the SFT training corpus. \\textbf{S2}~(Min-K\\%++): a membership inference statistic based on token-level surprisal \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp}. \\textbf{S3}~(ConStat): the accuracy gap between original and paraphrased item variants \\citep{dekoninck2024constat}. \\textbf{S4}~(Self-Critique): negative entropy of model self-evaluation distributions, inspired by the finding that LLMs can assess their own knowledge \\citep{kadavath2022}; concurrent work by \\citet{tao2025detecting} uses a related but mechanistically distinct entropy collapse approach targeting RL-trained models. S3 requires paired paraphrased items as reference, limiting applicability to benchmarks with existing paraphrase sets. Our primary pipeline uses the remaining three signals ($\\mathcal{S} = \\{\\text{S1, S2, S4}\\}$), making it applicable to any benchmark without auxiliary data requirements. S2 and S4 exhibit known failure modes under RL post-training, which we characterize in \\S\\ref{sec:signal_failure}.\n\nWe aggregate signals via Reciprocal Rank Fusion \\citep[RRF;][]{cormack_2009_rrf}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:rrf}\n    e_{ij} = \\sum_{s \\in \\mathcal{S}} \\frac{1}{\\kappa + r_{s}(i)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $r_s(i)$ is the rank of item~$i$ under signal~$s$ (rank~1 = most suspicious), $\\kappa=60$ is the standard smoothing constant, and $|\\mathcal{S}|=3$ in our primary configuration. Raw scores are min-max normalized per model to obtain $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$.\n\nRRF is a deliberate design choice. A learned aggregator requires labeled contamination data, but ground-truth labels are precisely what we lack. The aggregator's own training data may be contaminated, creating a meta-circularity problem. RRF sidesteps this by operating on rank orderings without parameter estimation.\n\n\\subsection{Stage 2: Contamination-Augmented IRT}\n\\label{sec:stage2}\n\n\\paragraph{Primary model: 2PL+$\\gamma$.}\nOur primary specification augments the two-parameter logistic model with a contamination covariate. We stratify items by cognitive domain and fit independent IRT models per stratum, yielding domain-specific $\\gamma_k$ estimates. This avoids cross-domain item parameter borrowing and permits heterogeneous contamination effects without imposing a shared latent structure. For stratum~$k$ (e.g., one of MMLU's 8 subject domains), the response probability is:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:2pl_gamma}\n    P(y_{ijk} = 1) = \\sigma\\!\\bigl(a_i(\\theta_j - b_i) + \\gamma_k \\cdot e_{ij}\\bigr),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(\\cdot)$ is the logistic function, $a_i > 0$ is item discrimination, $b_i \\in \\mathbb{R}$ is item difficulty, $\\theta_j$ is model ability, and $\\gamma_k$ is the scalar contamination effect for stratum~$k$. Each stratum is fitted independently: memorization may inflate knowledge recall while leaving reasoning items unaffected, and the independent fitting design captures this heterogeneity without structural assumptions. When a benchmark lacks domain structure, a single stratum encompassing all items is fitted.\n\nThe additive structure admits a natural interpretation: contamination shifts the effective difficulty of item~$i$ by $\\gamma_k \\cdot e_{ij} / a_i$ units on the ability scale.\n\nFor MC-format benchmarks, a 4PL extension adds guessing and ceiling asymptotes (Appendix~\\ref{sec:4pl_details}); this requires $J \\geq 16$ and is not used in our primary $J = 13$ analyses.\n\n\\paragraph{Inference.}\nWe perform Bayesian inference via NUTS \\citep{hoffman2014} in Stan \\citep{carpenter2017}, running four chains with target acceptance 0.95. Priors: $\\theta_j \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$, $a_i \\sim \\text{LogNormal}(0, 0.5)$, $b_i \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$, $\\gamma_k \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$. Convergence requires split-$\\hat{R} < 1.01$ and bulk ESS~$> 400$ for all parameters.\n\n\\paragraph{Calibrated score.}\nThe decontaminated accuracy for model~$j$ on benchmark~$k$ is the posterior expectation under zero exposure:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:calibrated}\n    \\text{Acc}_{j}^{\\text{cal}} = \\frac{1}{I_k} \\sum_{i=1}^{I_k}\\, \\mathbb{E}_{\\boldsymbol{\\psi} \\sim p(\\boldsymbol{\\psi} \\mid \\mathbf{y})} \\!\\left[\\sigma\\!\\bigl(a_i(\\theta_j - b_i)\\bigr)\\right],\n\\end{equation}\nwith credible intervals from posterior quantiles. Models with noisier exposure signals receive wider intervals by construction.\n\n\\subsection{Fisher Information and Identifiability}\n\\label{sec:fisher}\n\nThe reliability of $\\gamma$ estimation depends on two factors: signal discriminative quality (AUC of exposure scores) and ensemble size~$J$. The expected Fisher information $\\mathcal{I}(\\gamma)$ depends on $\\text{Var}(e_{\\cdot i})$ across models for each item (full derivation in Appendix~A). When signal AUC is low, exposure scores become uniform, collapsing this variance and rendering $\\gamma$ structurally unidentifiable.\n\nWe validate via simulation (20 conditions, 200 runs; details in \\S\\ref{sec:identifiability}). Recovery succeeds down to AUC$=$0.55 with $J \\geq 50$, but current LLM evaluation pools $J = 8$--$30$ \\citep{habba_2025_growing_pains,lalor_2024_irt_nlp}. At our $J = 13$, $\\theta$ estimation remains well-calibrated, but per-domain $\\gamma$ diagnosis requires larger model pools.\n\n\\subsection{Calibration Value Decomposition}\n\\label{sec:value_decomposition}\n\nEq.~\\eqref{eq:calibrated} depends on $\\theta_j$, not on $\\gamma$ directly. Two mechanisms contribute: (a)~IRT ability estimation, which separates stable capability from item-level noise regardless of the contamination model; and (b)~the explicit $\\gamma$ correction, which absorbs contamination variance during joint estimation. The decomposition predicts that vanilla 2PL ($\\gamma\\!=\\!0$) should achieve comparable calibration to 2PL+$\\gamma$, with $\\gamma_k$ serving a complementary diagnostic role. We verify this empirically in \\S\\ref{sec:calibration_results}.",

          " ",
          " \\textit{Adaptation~2: Binary leakage $\\to$ continuous exposure.} Item compromise IRT uses binary indicators \\citep{sinharay_2017_preknowledge}. LLM contamination is graded: a model may have encountered an item zero, once, or hundreds of times. We replace binary indicators with continuous exposure scores $e_{ij} \\in [0,1]$ derived from detection signals, following the explanatory IRT tradition \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}.",
          " ",
          "-\\textit{Adaptation~3: Stable ability $\\to$ regime-conditional ability.} In educational testing, ability is approximately constant. For LLMs, the training regime (SFT vs.\\ RL) qualitatively changes both internal representations and signal reliability. We recover clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting exposure to zero) rather than regime-specific ability parameters.",
          "+\\textit{Adaptation~3: Stable ability $\\to$ regime-conditional ability.} In educational testing, ability is approximately constant. For LLMs, the training regime (supervised fine-tuning, SFT, vs.\\ reinforcement learning, RL) qualitatively changes both internal representations and signal reliability. We recover clean ability via counterfactual inference (setting exposure to zero) rather than regime-specific ability parameters.",
          " ",

7	We construct a controlled contamination testbed using Qwen2.5 \citep{qwen2024qwen25} at three scales (0.5B, 1.5B, 3B parameters). For each scale, we fine-tune with LoRA ($\alpha=32$, $r=16$, lr$=5\times10^{-5}$) on a general-purpose SFT corpus into which benchmark items are injected at four dosage levels: d000 (0\%, clean control), d005 (5\%), d025 (25\%), and d100 (100\%). Injected items are formatted as instruction-response pairs and pass a quality-control filter that removes ambiguous or malformed entries.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Controlled SFT Injection}\n\\label{sec:injection}\n\nWe construct a controlled contamination testbed using Qwen2.5 \\citep{qwen2024qwen25} at three scales (0.5B, 1.5B, 3B parameters). For each scale, we fine-tune with LoRA ($\\alpha=32$, $r=16$, lr$=5\\times10^{-5}$) on a general-purpose SFT corpus into which benchmark items are injected at four dosage levels: d000 (0\\%, clean control), d005 (5\\%), d025 (25\\%), and d100 (100\\%). Injected items are formatted as instruction-response pairs and pass a quality-control filter that removes ambiguous or malformed entries.\n\nEach configuration is trained with two random seeds (s42, s123), yielding 24 models across $3 \\times 4 \\times 2$ conditions. This within-family design isolates contamination dosage as the primary experimental variable while controlling for architecture, tokenizer, and pretraining data.\n\nWe exclude 0.5B models from MMLU and ARC IRT analyses due to answer-position bias that correlates with dosage (A-option rate: 46\\% at d000 to 98\\% at d100). Three 3B checkpoints are excluded for pipeline inconsistencies (details in Appendix~\\ref{sec:exclusion_details}), yielding $J=13$ respondents (1.5B$\\times$8 + 3B$\\times$5). The eight 1.5B d000 models serve as the clean reference anchor; 3B ability estimates are anchored via joint fitting with these controls. GSM8K retains all scales.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Family Analysis}\n\\label{sec:cross_family}\n\nTo test whether IRT calibration is robust to inclusion of new model families, we fine-tune Llama-3.2-3B \\citep{grattafiori2024llama3} under the same protocol (d000, d005, d025, d100) with a single seed, yielding $J=4$ additional models. This analysis is \\textbf{exploratory}: the single-seed design provides limited statistical power, and we report post-hoc power calculations in Appendix~E using Qwen $J=13$ effect sizes as reference.\n\nLlama-3.2-3B at d100 on MMLU exhibits catastrophic format collapse (all outputs default to option ``A'', accuracy $=0.34$) and is excluded from IRT fitting. ARC and GSM8K at d100 behave normally; the effective Llama MMLU dosage range is therefore d000 to d025.\n\n\\subsection{Benchmarks, Domains, and Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:benchmarks}\n\nWe evaluate on three benchmarks spanning distinct cognitive demands and response formats.\n\n\\textbf{MMLU} \\citep{hendrycks2021mmlu}: 14,042 multiple-choice items across 57 subjects, grouped into 8 cognitive domains (Math, Science, CS \\& Engineering, Medical, Humanities, Social Sciences, Business \\& Law, Other). This stratification reveals within-benchmark heterogeneity that aggregate scoring obscures (\\S\\ref{sec:results}).\n\n\\textbf{ARC-Challenge} \\citep{clark2018arc}: 1,172 multiple-choice science reasoning items.\n\n\\textbf{GSM8K} \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k}: 1,319 open-ended math problems whose free-form generation format avoids multiple-choice artifacts.\n\nWe assess calibration quality with six metrics: (1)~mean absolute error (MAE) between calibrated and ground-truth clean accuracy; (2)~Kendall $\\tau$ for rank preservation; (3)~credible interval coverage at 50\\%, 80\\%, and 95\\% levels; (4)~posterior predictive $p$-value (PPP $\\in [0.1, 0.9]$ indicates acceptable fit); (5)~$\\gamma$ significance (whether the 95\\% CI excludes zero); and (6)~split-$\\hat{R}$ convergence ($<1.01$).\n\n\\subsection{Baselines}\n\\label{sec:baselines}\n\nWe compare against seven baselines: \\textbf{B0-Raw} (no adjustment), \\textbf{B1-Remove\\&Rescore} (drop flagged items), \\textbf{B2-Single2PL+$\\gamma$} (aggregate $\\gamma$ without domain stratification), \\textbf{B3-Vanilla2PL} ($\\gamma=0$, isolating IRT ability estimation), \\textbf{B4-LinearRegression} (residuals on exposure scores), \\textbf{B5-Emperor} (mitigation strategies from \\citet{sun2025emperor}), and \\textbf{B6-DCR} \\citep{xu2025dcr} (qualitative comparison; no public implementation).",

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex:31:RRF is a deliberate design choice. A learned aggregator requires labeled contamination data, but ground-truth labels are precisely what we lack. The aggregator's own training data may be contaminated, creating a meta-circularity problem. RRF sidesteps this by operating on rank orderings without parameter estimation.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/abstract.tex:1:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/introduction.tex:9:[Omitted long matching line]
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/introduction.tex:14:    \item \textbf{Multi-signal exposure detection via RRF.} We develop a training-free signal fusion pipeline that combines embedding similarity, token-level surprisal, and self-critique signals into continuous per-item exposure scores applicable to any benchmark without auxiliary data requirements.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/results.tex:23:Figure~\ref{fig:signal_heatmap} summarizes detection AUC across signal-scale-dosage combinations. S1 (embedding similarity) achieves AUC $> 0.95$ at d005 and above for all scales. S4 (Self-Critique) performs near-random at 0.5B and reaches marginal discrimination at 1.5B+. RRF fusion degrades to S1-only performance because S2 and S4 contribute noise at small scales.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/results.tex:25:Cross-family analysis with Llama-3.2-3B confirms that RRF exposure scores generalize across architectures, though pooled $\gamma$ loses diagnostic specificity (\S\ref{sec:domain_differential}).

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex:62:The reliability of $\gamma$ estimation depends on two factors: signal discriminative quality (AUC of exposure scores) and ensemble size~$J$. The expected Fisher information $\mathcal{I}(\gamma)$ depends on $\text{Var}(e_{\cdot i})$ across models for each item (full derivation in Appendix~A). When signal AUC is low, exposure scores become uniform, collapsing this variance and rendering $\gamma$ structurally unidentifiable.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/method.tex:64:We validate via simulation (20 conditions, 200 runs; details in \S\ref{sec:identifiability}). At our $J = 13$, $\theta$ estimation remains well-calibrated, but per-domain $\gamma$ diagnosis requires larger model pools.

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01GCxM8EjjhZ22yJ7Zue1GJq`</summary>

```text
7	\paragraph{IRT for LLM evaluation.}
8	Item Response Theory has gained traction as a principled alternative to raw accuracy. \citet{lalor_2024_irt_nlp} provide a comprehensive IRT tutorial for NLP; TinyBenchmarks \citep{polo_2024_tinybenchmarks} uses IRT-informed item selection for efficient evaluation; Growing Pains \citep{habba_2025_growing_pains} validates IRT at scale on 400+ models and identifies low-respondent limitations; HiBayES \citep{luettgau_2025_hibayes} adds hierarchical Bayesian inference for credible intervals; ATLAS \citep{li_2024_atlas} applies IRT for adaptive testing. All treat items as equally trustworthy. ContamCal augments IRT with contamination covariates $\gamma_k$ within the item response function, drawing on the educational measurement tradition of item compromise modeling \citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge}, DIF cancellation \citep{ercikan_2008_dif_cancellation}, and explanatory IRT \citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}, with three adaptations for LLM evaluation: continuous exposure scores, domain-level effects, and counterfactual inference (\S\ref{sec:formulation}).
9	
```

</details>

{

  "parentUuid": "66c15451-4b89-4420-8937-19d31b9463df",

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_13/projects/contamcal-irt-calibration/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
    "oldString": "DIF cancellation \\citep{ercikan_2008_dif_cancellation}",
    "newString": "differential item functioning (DIF) cancellation \\citep{ercikan_2008_dif_cancellation}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Contamination detection and mitigation.}\nMembership inference approaches include Min-K\\% \\citep{shi_2023_minkprob} and Min-K\\%++ \\citep{zhang_2024_minkpp} (token-level surprisal), ConStat \\citep{dekoninck2024constat} (accuracy gaps on paraphrased items), CDD/TED \\citep{dong_2024_cdd} (completion-based probes), KDS \\citep{choi_2025_kds} (kernel divergence), and TS-Guessing \\citep{deng_2024_tsguessing} (temporal splits). Concurrent with our work, \\citet{tao2025detecting} propose an entropy collapse method for RL-trained models. These methods flag contamination but do not produce adjusted scores. On the mitigation side, \\citet{sun2025emperor} systematically evaluate twenty approaches (rephrasing, option shuffling, dynamic construction) and find that none reliably restores clean performance, motivating post-hoc correction. DCR \\citep{xu2025dcr} estimates decontaminated scores via fuzzy inference but produces point estimates without uncertainty quantification or domain-level diagnosis. ContamCal consumes detection signals as inputs and feeds them into a calibration framework that estimates decontaminated ability with credible intervals.\n\n\\paragraph{IRT for LLM evaluation.}\nItem Response Theory has gained traction as a principled alternative to raw accuracy. \\citet{lalor_2024_irt_nlp} provide a comprehensive IRT tutorial for NLP; TinyBenchmarks \\citep{polo_2024_tinybenchmarks} uses IRT-informed item selection for efficient evaluation; Growing Pains \\citep{habba_2025_growing_pains} validates IRT at scale on 400+ models and identifies low-respondent limitations; HiBayES \\citep{luettgau_2025_hibayes} adds hierarchical Bayesian inference for credible intervals; ATLAS \\citep{li_2024_atlas} applies IRT for adaptive testing. All treat items as equally trustworthy. ContamCal augments IRT with contamination covariates $\\gamma_k$ within the item response function, drawing on the educational measurement tradition of item compromise modeling \\citep{eckerly_2017_item_compromise,sinharay_2017_preknowledge}, DIF cancellation \\citep{ercikan_2008_dif_cancellation}, and explanatory IRT \\citep{deboeck_2004_explanatory_irt}, with three adaptations for LLM evaluation: continuous exposure scores, domain-level effects, and counterfactual inference (\\S\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Calibration disambiguation.}\nWe use ``calibration'' in the psychometric sense: adjusting scores to approximate clean (uncontaminated) performance. This differs from probability calibration \\citep{guo_2017_calibration}, where the goal is aligning predicted confidences with empirical frequencies. Our reliability diagrams compare calibrated scores against held-out clean accuracy, not predicted probabilities against observed outcomes.\n",

Concurrent with our work, \\citet{tao2025detecting} propose an entropy collapse method for RL-trained models. These methods flag contamination but do not produce adjusted scores. On the mitigation side, \\citet{sun2025emperor} systematically evaluate twenty approaches (rephrasing, option shuffling, dynamic construction) and find that none reliably restores clean performance, motivating post-hoc correction. _source line 3053 · 2026-05-17T11:50:21.992Z_

[Director 指令] **[紧急] Table 2 baseline 扩展 — 必须补全**

Manager 自查发现 Table 2 只有 B0/B3/ContamCal 三行，但 exp-006 已计算了更多 baseline 数据。审稿人会要求看完整对比。

exp-006 registry 中的 baseline MAE 数据：
- B0_raw: MAE=0.0034
- B3_vanilla_4pl: MAE=0.0123
- B4_linear_regression: MAE=0.003
- B6_dcr_fuzzy: MAE=0.2634
- B2_2pl_gamma: MAE=0.3202
- IRT_4pl_gamma (ContamCal): MAE=0.0195

注意：这些是 exp-006 的早期数据（可能是 0.5B GSM8K），不一定是 Table 2 当前 benchmark 的数据。

处理方式：
1. 检查这些 baseline MAE 数据对应哪个 benchmark/scale/N
2. 如果与当前 Table 2 的 ARC/GSM8K/MMLU 设置一致，直接填入
3. 如果不一致，在 Appendix 新建一个完整 baseline 对比表（7 baselines + ContamCal），注明数据来源
4. 无论如何，Table 2 caption 不能再说"详见正文/Appendix"而 Appendix 没有数值——要么正文有表，要么 Appendix 有表

DCR (B6) 是最关键的——它是唯一已有的 calibration 方法。即使 MAE=0.263（远差于 ContamCal），也必须展示。
